AI Sweden
Accelerate Industry Applications With LLMs
AI Sweden facilitated regional language model applications by providing easy access to a powerful 100 billion-parameter model. They digitized historical records to develop language models for commercial use.
Amazon
How Amazon and NVIDIA Help Sellers Create Better Product Listings With AI
Amazon doubles inference speeds for new AI capabilities using NVIDIA TensorRT-LLM and GPUs to help sellers optimize product listings faster.
Amdocs
NVIDIA and Amdocs Bring Custom Generative AI to Global Telco Industry
Amdocs plans to build custom LLMs for $1.7 trillion global telecommunications industry using NVIDIA AI foundry service on Microsoft Azure.
AT&T
AT&T Drives Customer Care AI Agents’ Accuracy, Efficiency, and Performance With NVIDIA NeMo
AT&T, one of the world’s largest telecommunications companies, is reimagining customer care through the power of AI. Facing challenges like model drift, rising computational demands, and the need for real-time data access, AT&T turned to NVIDIA NeMo™ microservices to build a feedback-driven AI platform that continuously improves performance while optimizing cost, speed, and compliance.
AWS
NVIDIA Powers Training for Some of the Largest Amazon Titan Foundation Models
Amazon leveraged the NVIDIA NeMo framework, GPUs, and AWS EFAs to train its next-generation LLM, giving some of the largest Amazon Titan foundation models customers a faster, more accessible solution for generative AI.
Accenture
Accelerate Generative AI Adoption for Enterprises
ServiceNow, NVIDIA, and Accenture announced the launch of AI Lighthouse, a first-of-its-kind program designed to fast-track the development and adoption of enterprise generative AI capabilities.
Azure
Harnessing the Power of NVIDIA AI Enterprise on Azure Machine Learning
Get access to a complete ecosystem of tools, libraries, frameworks, and support services tailored for enterprise environments on Microsoft Azure.
Bria
Bria Builds Responsible Generative AI for Enterprises Using NVIDIA NeMo, Picasso
Bria, a startup based in Tel Aviv, is helping businesses who are seeking responsible ways to integrate visual generative AI technology into their enterprise products with a generative AI service that emphasizes model transparency alongside fair attribution and copyright protections.
Cohesity
Unlock Your Data Superpower: NVIDIA Microservices Unleash Enterprise-Grade Secure Generative AI for Cohesity
With NVIDIA NIM and optimized models, Cohesity DataProtect customers can add generative AI intelligence to data backups and archives. This allows Cohesity and NVIDIA to bring the power of generative AI to all Cohesity DataProtect customers. Leveraging the power of NIM and NVIDIA optimized models, Cohesity DataProtect customers obtain the power of data-driven insights from their data backups and archives, unleashing new levels of efficiency, innovation, and growth.
CrowdStrike
Shaping the Future of AI in the Cybersecurity Domain
CrowdStrike and NVIDIA are leveraging accelerated computing and generative AI to provide customers with an innovative range of AI-powered solutions tailored to efficiently address security threats.
Dell
Dell Validated Design for Generative AI With NVIDIA
Dell Technologies and NVIDIA announced an initiative to make it easier for businesses to build and use generative AI models on premises quickly and securely.
Deloitte
Unlock the Value of Generative AI Across Enterprise Software Platforms
Deloitte will use NVIDIA AI technology and expertise to build high-performing generative AI solutions for enterprise software platforms to help unlock significant business value.
Domino Data Lab
Domino Offers Production-Ready Generative AI Powered by NVIDIA
With NVIDIA NeMo, data scientists can fine-tune LLMs in Domino’s platform for domain-specific use cases based on proprietary data and IP—without needing to start from scratch.
Dropbox
Dropbox and NVIDIA to Bring Personalized Generative AI to Millions of Customers
Dropbox plans to leverage NVIDIA’s AI foundry to build custom models and improve AI-powered knowledge work with Dropbox Dash universal search tool and Dropbox AI.
Google Cloud
AI Titans Collaborate to Create Generative AI Magic
At its Next conference, Google Cloud announced the availability of its A3 instances powered by NVIDIA H100 Tensor Core GPUs. Engineering teams from both companies have collaborated to bring NVIDIA NeMo to the A3 instances for faster training and inference.
Google Cloud Console
HuggingFace
Leading AI Community to Accelerate Data Curation Pipeline
Hugging Face, the leading open platform for AI builders, is collaborating with NVIDIA to integrate NeMo Curator and accelerate DataTrove, their data filtering and deduplication library. “We are excited about the GPU acceleration capabilities of NeMo Curator and can’t wait to see them contributed to DataTrove!” says Jeff Boudier, Product Director at Hugging Face.
KT
Creating New Customer Experiences With LLMs
South Korea’s leading mobile operator builds billion-parameter LLMs trained with the NVIDIA DGX SuperPOD platform and NeMo framework to power smart speakers and customer call centers.
Lenovo
New Reference Architecture for Generative AI Based on LLMs
Solution to expedite innovation by empowering global partners and customers to develop, train, and deploy AI at scale across industry verticals with utmost safety and efficiency.
Quantiphi
Enabling Enterprises to Fast-Track Their AI-Driven Journeys
Quantiphi specializes in training and fine-tuning foundation models using the NVIDIA NeMo framework, as well as optimizing deployments at scale with the NVIDIA AI Enterprise software platform, while adhering to responsible AI principles.
SAP
SAP and NVIDIA Accelerate Generative AI Adoption Across Enterprise Applications Powering Global Industries
Customers can harness their business data in cloud solutions from SAP using customized LLMs deployed with NVIDIA AI foundry services and NVIDIA NIM.
ServiceNow
Building Generative AI Across Enterprise IT
ServiceNow develops custom LLMs on its ServiceNow platform to enable intelligent workflow automation and boost productivity across enterprise IT processes.
Perplexity
Enhance Model Performance for AI-Powered Search Engines
Using NVIDIA NeMo, Perplexity aims to quickly customize frontier models to improve the accuracy and quality of search results and optimize them for lower latency and high throughput for a better user experience.
VMware
VMware and NVIDIA Unlock Generative AI for Enterprises
VMware Private AI Foundation with NVIDIA will enable enterprises to customize models and run generative AI applications, including intelligent chatbots, assistants, search, and summarization.
Weight & Biases
Debug, Optimize, and Monitor LLM Pipelines
Weights & Biases helps teams working on generative AI use cases or with LLMs track and visualize all prompt-engineering experiments—helping users debug and optimize LLM pipelines—as well as provides monitoring and observability capabilities for LLMs.
Writer
Startup Pens Generative AI Success Story With NVIDIA NeMo
Using NVIDIA NeMo, Writer is building LLMs that are helping hundreds of companies create custom content for enterprise use cases across marketing, training, support, and more.
Arize
Arize Powers Self-Improving AI Data Flywheels
Arize’s LLM engineering and observability platform integrates NVIDIA NeMo microservices to power AI data flywheels, enabling continuous model refinement through real-world feedback. With NeMo Customizer, Evaluator, and Guardrails, Arize ensures agentic systems are performant, safe, and aligned with evolving enterprise needs. This collaboration supports the development of adaptive AI that learns and evolves over time.
DataRobot
Enterprise-Ready, Trustworthy AI Agents With NeMo on DataRobot
With NVIDIA NeMo embedded into the DataRobot Enterprise AI Suite, enterprises can ensure agentic systems are safe, compliant, and grounded in enterprise-specific data. This integration facilitates the development of AI agents that deliver accurate, context-aware responses while adhering to organizational standards.
DataStax
DataStax and NVIDIA Build Data and AI Platform
Over the past year, DataStax has partnered with NVIDIA to adopt NVIDIA NeMo microservices to enhance generative AI, retrieval-augmented generation, and hybrid search across its database and AI offerings. The results have been impressive: 19x better performance in throughput, a significant reduction in costs, and improved latency.
Galileo
Galileo and NVIDIA NeMo: De-Risking Agentic AI in Production
Galileo integrates NVIDIA NeMo microservices to build AI data flywheels that strengthen agent performance, reliability, and trust. NeMo adds complementary capabilities to the Galileo platform—enabling continuous domain-specific fine-tuning via NeMo Customizer, advanced evaluation with NeMo Evaluator, and safeguarding user interactions with NeMo Guardrails to empower AI teams to build, evaluate, and monitor agentic AI systems that learn and improve continuously in real-world environments.

What Is Agentic AI?
Agentic AI
systems use autonomous, long-running agents to handle complex, multistep work—researching, deciding, and acting without waiting for human input at each step. In
multi-agent systems
, specialized agents divide the work, share information, and complete tasks in parallel.
Over time, these
AI agents
get sharper: Human and AI feedback continuously retrain models, so each workflow run improves the next.
Developers can build specialized enterprise AI agents with NVIDIA Agent Toolkit—open models, tools, skills, and libraries, along with a secure runtime that helps agents operate within boundaries enterprises define.
Open Models for Specialized AI Agents
Explore how NVIDIA Nemotron open models help AI agents reason across text and images, orchestrate workflows, and complete specialized tasks.
Read the Blog
Bring Text and Vision Together for AI Agents
NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Super VL combines text and visual understanding to help AI agents extract information from webpages, PDFs, tables, and charts and act on what they find.

Modular by Design
Everything enterprises need to build and run AI agents, designed to work together. Take the whole stack or layer in individual components—either way, you're building on a foundation that scales.
Trust Through Transparency

EDA Autonomous Engineer

Semiconductors
Autonomous EDA Engineer
Designing and verifying complex chips requires orchestrating dozens of specialized tools across months-long workflows—work that is slow, expensive, and bottlenecked by engineering capacity. With NVIDIA Agent Toolkit and NemoClaw, partners are building autonomous AI engineers that plan and execute multi-tool EDA workflows with no handoff, compressing weeks of RTL verification time to hours.
Learn More About Autonomous AI Engineers
All Industries
Traditional video analytics only detect what they were trained to find—missing the context and complexity of real-world operations. A new generation of highly accurate video analytics agents goes beyond detection to perceive, reason, and act on massive volumes of live and recorded video across factories, warehouses, retail stores, airports, and roadways. Built with
NVIDIA Metropolis VSS Blueprint
Cosmos
, these agents help operations teams make better decisions faster and unlock new possibilities for automation.
Learn More About Video Analytics Agents
Enterprise IT
Level 1 support is high volume, repetitive, and expensive—but requires careful governance and auditability. An IT service desk digital coworker built with NVIDIA Agent Toolkit can analyze incoming support tickets, investigate root causes using knowledge and historical data, and execute remediation across enterprise tools, completing entire workflows autonomously while maintaining full governance and control.
Learn More About IT Service Desk Agents
Healthcare and Life Sciences
AI agents are transforming how clinicians, researchers, and developers work, from automating complex workflows across drug discovery, clinical documentation, decision support, robotics, and hospital automation to patient engagement. Built on NVIDIA’s open model infrastructure, these agents aim to accelerate discoveries and improve outcomes at every stage of the care and discovery continuum.
Learn More About Biomolecular Generation
See How AI Agents Can Help Medical Professionals
Cybersecurity
Security teams face thousands of alerts daily with too little time to thoroughly investigate each one. Cyber defense digital coworkers built with NVIDIA Agent Toolkit and
Nemotron
reasoning models can autonomously monitor threats, triage incidents, and coordinate response—so analysts can focus on the highest-priority threats while agents handle the volume.

Frequently asked questions
Why do I need an identity layer?
Agents are a new kind of identity. They act on behalf of humans, make access decisions, and connect to APIs, SaaS apps, MCP servers, and other agents, often without a human in the loop.
Without an identity layer, most enterprises can’t:
See what their agents connect to
Ensure those connections are secure
Control what agents can do
Stop agents when something goes wrong
Without identity, most enterprises are left with gaps like:
No inventory of which agents exist, who owns them, or what they're authorized to do
No way to answer "show me every agent in our environment"
No record to produce when an auditor asks who authorized an action
Identity fixes this at the root:
A named owner tied to a human gives accountability
Permissions enforced at runtime give authorization that works
Logged actions against a verified identity give an audit trail
The ability to revoke identity gives containment
Runtime detection, access policies, and a kill switch all depend on this foundation
How does Okta tackle AI agent identity security differently from other vendors?
Okta is widely recognized as a market leader in AI agent identity security and non-human identity management, backed by over 20,000 enterprise customers and consistent Gartner Magic Quadrant Leader recognition in Access Management.
Unlike legacy identity providers or cloud-specific vendors whose tools are confined to single ecosystems, Okta provides an open, vendor-neutral control plane that spans multi-cloud environments, heterogeneous SaaS platforms, and custom developer architectures.
Okta’s AI agent security stack is built around three foundational capabilities across workforce and customer identity use cases:
Agent Single Sign-On (Agent SSO):
Makes the open Cross App Access standard part of core Okta SSO, giving every Okta customer a first-class identity model for AI agents. Okta for AI Agents extends discovery, lifecycle management, and governance to every agent in the enterprise.
Cross App Access (XAA):
An enterprise-grade implementation of the Model Context Protocol (MCP) that issues short-lived, scoped tokens for cross-application and agent-to-agent task delegation.
Fine-Grained Authorization (FGA):
Relationship-based access control (ReBAC) that evaluates and enforces permissions at the individual tool-call level in real time.
What are the top security risks of deploying AI agents?
The primary security risks of deploying autonomous AI agents center on excessive agency, credential sprawl, and lack of runtime visibility, which can allow unmanaged agents to access sensitive enterprise data or execute unauthorized operation
According to the
OWASP Top 10 for Large Language Models and Generative AI
, the top security threats in agentic architectures include:
Excessive Agency (ASI01 / LLM06):
Autonomous agents granted overly broad permissions, allowing them to read, write, or execute destructive actions across enterprise tools beyond their intended operational scope.
Tool Poisoning & Insecure Tool Execution:
Malicious or malformed inputs manipulating an agent into executing unauthorized API calls or exfiltrating data via third-party integrations.
Memory & Context Injection:
Compromising an agent’s persistent memory or runtime context to alter its behavioral constraints and bypass safety guardrails.
Shadow AI & Standing Credential Sprawl:
Enterprise teams spinning up unvetted agents with hardcoded, long-lived API keys that bypass IT governance and security audits.
Analysts project that the average enterprise will deploy over 150,000 autonomous agents by 2028, yet only 13% of security leaders currently possess the tools to govern and audit non-human identities effectively.*
What should I look for in an identity layer as part of my agentic security strategy?
To fully support agentic AI workflows, an identity platform must provide a unified control plane capable of governing non-human identities across five essential technical criteria: discovery, scoped credentialing, runtime authorization, secure multi-agent delegation, and automated governance audit trails.
The 5 essential capabilities for Agentic IAM:
Agent registration & discovery
: Automatically surface unsanctioned (shadow) agents and register them into a central directory with explicit human accountability.
Short-lived, scoped token issuance:
Eliminate static API keys by issuing dynamic, least-privilege tokens, not broadly scoped permissions.
Runtime authorization enforcement:
Inspect and approve individual tool calls at runtime based on user context and policy constraints.
Secure multi-agent handoffs:
Facilitate trusted context and authorization transfer when a primary agent delegates sub-tasks to downstream agents.
Continuous auditing & threat containment:
Log each access decision, stream to your SIEM, and enforce a “kill-switch” if an agent deviates from its baseline behavior.
Okta satisfies all five requirements and is a founding member of the Blueprint Alliance, an open ecosystem encompassing 25+ major technology and security partners.
How do you secure the full AI agent lifecycle?
Securing AI agents across their full lifecycle requires a structured, three-phase framework: Discover, Onboard, and Govern & Protect.
The 3-phase implementation framework:
Phase 1: Discover (visibility & risk assessment)
Deploy Identity Security Posture Management (ISPM) to continuously scan environments for shadow AI agents and their connections. Map all active connections to establish an authoritative inventory of discovered agents.
Phase 2: Onboard (machine authentication & scoped access)
Register each agent in the Universal Directory using Agent SSO. Replace long-lived API credentials with short-lived, cryptographic access tokens managed via Cross App Access (XAA), keeping each agent’s blast radius scoped from the start
Phase 3: Govern & Protect (runtime control & audit)
Enforce continuous least privilege through automated access certifications and configure real-time policy triggers to revoke access for agents when things go wrong.
What is Cross App Access (XAA) and why does it matter for AI agents?
Cross App Access (XAA) is an open identity standard and security protocol designed to govern how autonomous AI agents authenticate and interact with enterprise SaaS applications, APIs, and downstream sub-agents without relying on standing credentials.
Traditional integrations rely on long-lived API keys or static OAuth tokens, which create massive security vulnerabilities when autonomous agents execute multi-step workflows across disparate applications.
Eliminates credential sprawl:
Instead of storing persistent secrets inside agent code, XAA generates ephemeral, tightly scoped tokens on demand for specific tool calls.
Standardizes multi-agent delegation:
Built on the Enterprise-Managed Authorization extension for the Model Context Protocol (MCP), XAA serves as the universal interoperability standard for secure agent-to-app and agent-to-agent communication.
Maintains user & policy context:
XAA preserves verified user identity context throughout asynchronous, chained workflows, ensuring agents can never access resources that the delegating human user is not authorized to see.
Enterprise Interoperability:
As of 2026, XAA supports native integrations across 25+ major cloud, developer, and enterprise SaaS platforms.
How should organizations start governing AI agents?
Organizations should begin governing AI agents by executing a diagnostic evaluation based on the Blueprint for the Secure Agentic Enterprise, which establishes operational control across four core stages: Discovery, Scope, Runtime Monitoring, and Active Containment.
The 4 core diagnostic checkpoints:
(Discovery & Visibility): Audit the environment to identify every running agent, its deployment environment, its codebase origin, and its assigned human owner.
(Scope & Blast Radius): Document and constrain the exact SaaS tools, databases, and APIs each agent is authorized to interact with, eliminating standing administrative privileges.
(Runtime Authorization & Auditing): Implement policy checkpoints at every tool invocation to verify authorization before an agent executes an API call or reads sensitive data.
(Active Containment & Revocation): Establish automated incident response protocols and kill-switch capabilities to instantly isolate compromised agents, revoke credentials, and roll back unintended state changes.
Your agents are ready. Is your security?
Take the next step in securing your AI agents with a guided walkthrough from our specialists.
Talk to an expert
*Gartner: “
Gartner Identifies Six Steps to Manage AI Agent Sprawl
” (Aug. 28, 2026).

Born in the unforgiving world of safety-critical systems, OmniTrust secures the chain of trust from silicon to AI, protecting devices, identities, and intelligence throughout their lifecycles. When failure threatens safety, disrupts operations, or undermines confidence, the world’s critical industries turn to OmniTrust to secure and protect what matters.
Our Platform
Trust Lifecycle Management
OmniTrust’s Trust Lifecycle Management (TLM) Platform secures the connected world from the inside out. It begins where trust is born – inside the silicon of your connected device footprint – and extends across machines, clouds, apps, humans, and AI.
Discovers hidden assets
Defends them with cryptographic strength
Governs their lifecycle from design to decommission
Provides trust that is continuous, adaptive, and uncompromising
Secures the entire spectrum of modern ecosystems
Explore the Platform
Partners & Customers
Industry Case Studies
See how the world’s most critical industries rely on OmniTrust to secure devices, systems, and ecosystems across every lifecycle.
View More
Automotive & Transportation
Aerospace & Defense
Energy, Oil & Gas
Power & Utilities
Financial Services
OmniTrust secures the automotive and transportation ecosystem across the full vehicle and infrastructure lifecycle, from silicon and manufacturing through deployment, operation, and service. Supporting OEMs, Tier-1 and Tier-2 suppliers, fleets, and mobility infrastructure, OmniTrust delivers cryptographic trust, secure software updates, and compliance-aligned assurance that keeps modern mobility systems safe, resilient, and trustworthy at scale.
OmniTrust secures aerospace, defense, and weapons systems across their full operational lifecycle, from design and manufacturing through deployment and sustainment. Backed by decades of domain expertise, OmniTrust delivers cryptographic trust, post-quantum readiness, and compliance-aligned assurance to ensure mission platforms remain authenticated, resilient, and mission-ready in the most demanding environments.
OmniTrust secures critical energy infrastructure across exploration, production, and distribution. It delivers end-to-end lifecycle trust for SCADA, PLC, and smart grid systems - protecting assets and data with cryptographic assurance, post-quantum resilience, and continuous compliance across global operations.
OmniTrust secures power and utility operators by establishing trusted identities for grid assets, enforcing cryptographic integrity on firmware and configurations, and automating credential governance across operational systems. Continuous trust monitoring reveals weak or tampered components early, ensuring resilient operations, regulatory compliance, and verifiable security across generation, transmission, and distribution environments.
OmniTrust protects financial institutions by securing identities, transactions, and distributed infrastructure with strong cryptographic integrity. Automated lifecycle governance eliminates credential failures, detects unauthorized changes, and verifies provenance - supporting resilient operations and regulatory compliance across payments, trading systems, ATMs, branch networks, and cloud-based financial services.
Featured Solutions
Connected & Smart Cities Trust Infrastructure
Establish verifiable trust across transportation, energy, and civic networks. Authenticate devices, secure data exchange, and ensure NIS2 and UNECE compliance for citywide digital infrastructure and mobility systems.
Secure Supply Chain & Manufacturing Trust
Guarantee authenticity and provenance from design to deployment. Enforce cryptographic credentialing, signed firmware, and traceable CBOM lineage across OEMs, suppliers, and production ecosystems.
Zero-Trust Identity & Credential Management
Unify certificate, key, and identity lifecycle automation across enterprise, cloud, and device domains. Strengthen access control, enforce crypto policy, and maintain compliance with NIS2 and ISO 27001.
Post-Quantum & Crypto Agility Readiness
Discover, assess, and transition cryptographic assets to quantum-safe algorithms. Automate CBOM generation, crypto inventory, and posture analytics aligned with NIST PQC and EU CRA requirements.
Lifecycle Compliance & Regulatory Assurance​
Automate continuous cryptographic compliance for NIS2, eIDAS2, ISO, and sector frameworks. Deliver auditable trust evidence, policy alignment, and lifecycle reporting across embedded and enterprise systems.
Secure Software & Firmware Signing​
Ensure authenticity of every release through centralized signing and policy enforcement. Protect CI/CD, OTA, and runtime environments with verifiable cryptographic assurance and regulatory compliance.
AI & Agent Identity Assurance
Issue and govern cryptographic identities for AI models and autonomous agents. Enforce verifiable credentials, policy-controlled access, and auditability for AI governance under EU and global standards.
Trust Intelligence & Analytics
Gain unified visibility into cryptographic health, lifecycle hygiene, and policy compliance. Deliver real-time analytics and reporting across DLM, and ILM environments for proactive risk management.

Create production-ready AI assistants, autonomous agents, and multi-agent teams with simple natural language instructions—no coding required. Choose your preferred models, frameworks, skills, RAG sources, and deployment options, then launch with a single click. Halo Agent Studio transforms ideas into trusted AI workers using prompt-driven configuration, reusable skills, knowledge libraries, and enterprise-ready templates that dramatically reduce development time while remaining fully customizable for advanced teams.
Open Models & Frameworks
Avoid vendor lock-in by building with virtually any AI ecosystem. Halo Agent Studio supports frontier and open-weight models, private AI deployments, and popular frameworks including OpenAI Agents, Anthropic, Google ADK, CrewAI, LangGraph, AutoGen, N8N, OpenClaw, Microsoft Copilot Studio, and custom enterprise frameworks—giving organizations complete flexibility as AI technologies evolve.
Enterprise Integration & Automation
Connect AI agents to enterprise applications, APIs, databases, SaaS platforms, documents, and business workflows through reusable connectors and secure integrations. Agents can retrieve enterprise knowledge, automate repetitive tasks, orchestrate complex business processes, and collaborate across departments while remaining fully integrated with existing enterprise technology investments.
Trust-Native Runtime Security
Every AI agent is protected by Halo’s Trust-Native AI architecture, providing runtime policy enforcement, trusted AI identities, guardrails, audit trails, governance, and operational visibility. Organizations can safely deploy AI at enterprise scale while maintaining security, compliance, cost controls, and executive oversight—giving CIOs and CISOs confidence without slowing innovation.
Related Solution & Expert Services
Take the PKI maturity self assessment
Enterprise AI Discovery & Shadow AI Control
OmniTrust ALM, powered by Halo, brings decades of cybersecurity and specialized asset discovery expertise to enterprise AI. Using endpoint, browser, network and environment-level discovery, Halo identifies AI applications, LLMs, models, agents, APIs and tools, including Shadow AI and AI embedded within applications. It creates a continuously updated AI system of record, then applies centralized policy and runtime controls, giving organizations the visibility, evidence and enforcement foundation needed to govern and securely scale AI adoption.
Explore Solution
Leverage ALM's QuickStart Services, Strategy and Forward Deployed Engineers to prioritize and mobilize agent workforces in any area
Explore Services
Applicable to any industry seeking to bring secure and safe AI Workers, overseen by business teams in any functional area
Aerospace & Defense
OmniTrust secures aerospace, defense, and weapons systems across their full operational lifecycle, from design and manufacturing through deployment and sustainment. Backed by decades of domain expertise, OmniTrust delivers cryptographic trust, post-quantum readiness, and compliance-aligned assurance to ensure mission platforms remain authenticated, resilient, and mission-ready in the most demanding environments.
Automotive & Transportation
OmniTrust secures the automotive and transportation ecosystem across the full vehicle and infrastructure lifecycle, from silicon and manufacturing through deployment, operation, and service. Supporting OEMs, Tier-1 and Tier-2 suppliers, fleets, and mobility infrastructure, OmniTrust delivers cryptographic trust, secure software updates, and compliance-aligned assurance that keeps modern mobility systems safe, resilient, and trustworthy at scale.
Energy, Oil & Gas
OmniTrust secures critical energy infrastructure across exploration, production, and distribution. It delivers end-to-end lifecycle trust for SCADA, PLC, and smart grid systems – protecting assets and data with cryptographic assurance, post-quantum resilience, and continuous compliance across global operations.
Financial Services
OmniTrust protects financial institutions by securing identities, transactions, and distributed infrastructure with strong cryptographic integrity. Automated lifecycle governance eliminates credential failures, detects unauthorized changes, and verifies provenance – supporting resilient operations and regulatory compliance across payments, trading systems, ATMs, branch networks, and cloud-based financial services.
Healthcare & MedTech
OmniTrust secures connected medical devices, digital health platforms, and healthcare systems across their full operational lifecycle. From device design and manufacturing to clinical deployment and patient care, OmniTrust delivers embedded cryptographic trust, lifecycle governance, and regulatory-aligned assurance to ensure implants, diagnostics, connected equipment, and AI-driven systems remain safe, traceable, and compliant in highly regulated environments.
Industrial, Manufacturing & OT
OmniTrust secures industrial, manufacturing, and operational technology (OT) environments across their full lifecycle, from embedded controllers and factory floors to global networks and industrial platforms. Supporting manufacturers, infrastructure providers, and critical operations, OmniTrust delivers cryptographic trust, supply chain visibility, post-quantum readiness, and lifecycle assurance that keeps industrial systems resilient, compliant, and operational at scale.

The Challenges
Connected city infrastructure depends on trusted communication between vehicles, roadside systems, cloud platforms, and operational networks. Without authenticated identity, interoperable trust infrastructure, and verifiable V2X communications, transportation ecosystems face spoofing risk, operational disruption, and increasing compliance pressure under frameworks such as NIS2, UNECE WP.29, and ISO 21177.
Our Solutions
OmniTrust TLM delivers complete digital trust for connected cities and mobility ecosystems. Together, OmniTrust solutions establish verifiable identity, policy-controlled data exchange, and end-to-end cryptographic governance across transportation, infrastructure, and civic systems. This includes TrafficAuth, OmniTrust’s connected transportation trust platform, which provides secure V2X infrastructure, SCMS services, and interoperable Network + Direct V2X communications for smart city deployments.

## OpenAI - Patch the Planet: global response coordination as agents uncover critical vulnerabilities Security Oct 21, 2026

Source: https://openai.com/index/coordinated-disclosure-of-critical-vulnerabilities/
Captured: 2026-11-13
Content hash: 2f041c00af5200aae6e1f9f41a42caaa9a9b1d4be76256854b94fcb41dba9933

OpenAI
October 21, 2026
Security
Product
Patch the Planet: global response coordination as agents uncover critical vulnerabilities
Share
Coordinating a global response
Giving maintainers the capacity and resources to respond
Building more secure software foundations
Help us improve this approach
Over the past two months, we led a coalition of maintainers, researchers, and security providers to address critical vulnerabilities in PHP, an open-source programming language used to run websites and web applications. Calif identified a critical flaw using GPT‑5.6 Sol as part of our
Patch the Planet
⁠
(opens in a new window)
initiative, with Wiz also independently discovering and reporting both vulnerabilities. PHP’s maintainers led the fixes and release, with our teams and partners like Wiz, Cloudflare, and others contributing research, technical support, and coordination to prepare protections ahead of disclosure.
For organizations using PHP, the priority now is applying the fixes.
We’re sharing a PHP version checker as a Codex skill
to help operators identify potentially vulnerable PHP versions and, alongside PHP’s and their providers’ guidance, determine which updates apply.
Given the severity of these flaws and PHP’s widespread use, we consider this one of the most impactful security issues in recent memory. It highlights how AI can uncover security flaws with internet-scale impact, but the same capabilities can also help attackers rapidly develop offensive tools and exploits.
To our knowledge, frontier AI found these vulnerabilities before attackers did, opening a
Defender’s Window
for maintainers and security providers to prepare fixes and protections ahead of disclosure.
To help more defenders use these capabilities, we’ve committed $1 billion in subsidized
Daybreak access
through
Daybreak for Frontline Defenders
, focused on organizations with limited security resources protecting essential services.
As significant as they are, these bugs are among several significant vulnerabilities we’ve already found and are working to fix, and we expect similar findings to increase sharply in the coming months. Drawing on our experience thus far, we’re sharing a proposed approach, appropriate for the AI era, to responding to critical vulnerabilities in widely used software, and we’re asking the security community to help improve this framework. It brings together our models, security expertise, and maintainers to help turn findings into protection and build a response that can scale as AI capabilities advance.
Getting a fix into use can mean adapting it for older releases, preparing provider mitigations, and updating systems across many organizations. A repeatable process helps those teams act quickly as more vulnerabilities are discovered.
Through Patch the Planet, we contributed security expertise and model access to support the PHP response, and worked with maintainers and research partners to test fixes and prepare guidance. We also helped coordinate with infrastructure providers on protections for affected systems and engaged government cyber agencies to help guidance reach critical organizations. This supported the maintainer-led response and helped other defenders prepare.
For some findings, researchers can work directly with the software’s maintainers to investigate the issue and develop a fix. Others need a wider response because they affect many products or because distributors and service providers need time (which may be in short supply if frontier capabilities are not too far ahead of those that are broadly available) to prepare updates and protections. In those cases, the proposed framework can help maintainers identify who needs to be involved and coordinate the work. The response can involve five stages:
Validate and fix the upstream vulnerability.
The maintainer, researchers, and essential technical partners confirm the finding, assess its impact, and develop and test a fix. This process includes adversarial testing with frontier models to look for ways around the patch and related variants of the vulnerability.
Give affected organizations time to act.
We propose a framework for considering which organizations need advance information to prepare protections, while maintainers decide whom to involve. We help with outreach, information-sharing agreements, and coordination between teams. A distributor might adapt a fix for an older release, a hosting provider might test a mitigation, and a security provider might prepare detection or prevention measures, including web application firewall (WAF) rules.
Coordinate with government partners to help protect critical systems.
Government agencies play an essential role in coordinating a wider response and helping security guidance reach organizations responsible for critical infrastructure and other essential services. In the case of PHP, we worked, in collaboration with our partners, to engage with multiple parts of the U.S. government, including the White House’s Super Intelligence Force, the Office of the National Cyber Director (ONCD), the Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency (CISA), as well as international partners to support that effort.
Prepare updates across affected products.
As the fix, guidance, and disclosure plans become stable, maintainers can involve additional distributors, hosting and service providers, response teams, and operators to prepare updates and address gaps in coverage.
Once a patch is available, publish guidance and support deployment.
Public guidance helps people assess their exposure, find authoritative updates or interim mitigations, and check that a fix is running. The response continues as operators deploy updates and report where they need help.
The proposed framework considers an organization’s protective role, including protecting its own systems and customers, the time needed to act, and its ability to handle sensitive information. The same criteria would apply to comparable organizations, with maintainers deciding whom to involve ahead of disclosure.
Each participant receives the information needed to carry out their protective work, from network logs with indicators of compromise to more sensitive working proof-of-concept exploits. Even limited details can reveal a vulnerability, so we weigh the risk of sharing them against the protection they enable.
Teams following this framework would work with maintainers to give defenders time to prepare and reassess the timeline if there’s active exploitation in the wild, a leak, a revealing public patch, or increased risk to users from stalled remediation.
The
Patch the Planet website
brings together public disclosures and guidance to help operators identify affected systems, find the right update, and verify that the updated software is running properly. For PHP, it points to the maintainers’ official patch and upgrade instructions and our partners’ technical research. The guidance also covers interim mitigations, when to involve a provider or expert, and backup and recovery. Interim protections can reduce risk, but we’ll make their limits and operational tradeoffs clear.
Operators can use
CISA’s guidance on prioritization and remediation
to assess how quickly to act based on exposure, exploitation, and potential impact. That may mean emergency patching, immediate mitigation, or a planned update, alongside forensic triage where warranted to assess whether systems have already been compromised. Priorities should be reassessed as conditions change, with a clear path to lasting remediation.
Global coordination depends on the people who maintain the software. Many projects are run by small teams that review security reports alongside routine development. Those teams need help separating new issues from duplicates, assessing impact, and testing fixes across supported releases. We support that work with funding, access to capable models, and hands-on engineering help.
Powerful AI capabilities are advancing across the industry, and we expect AI to play a growing role in helping defenders find vulnerabilities, monitor systems, and respond to threats. We’re investing in models, tools, and partnerships to help defenders put those capabilities to work.
Our investments include subscriptions for all maintainers through
Codex for OSS
and direct sponsorship of projects such as the
Rust Foundation
for maintainers and technical work. Each project sets its own priorities. Reusable tests and security workflows can reduce the effort needed to handle future findings. Open-source projects can
apply to Patch the Planet
for hands-on support.
We’re also calibrating additional blocking classifiers for Daybreak to help reduce the potential for misuse. This involves difficult tradeoffs: safeguards intended to prevent abuse can also block legitimate security work. Broad access to capable models and effective protections against misuse are both critical. Our aim is to preserve the capabilities defenders need to find and fix vulnerabilities while limiting harmful use. We’ll continue refining that balance as capabilities advance and we learn from how these tools are used.
As models become more capable, the opportunity extends beyond finding and fixing individual vulnerabilities. We want to help engineers build more secure software from the start. Improvements to a widely used component can prevent bugs or limit their impact across many products and services.
AI can help engineers understand existing code, prepare changes, and develop tests, while maintainers and security experts verify that those changes preserve the behavior users depend on. We see opportunities in several areas:
Secure code from the start.
Coding models can help developers choose safer defaults, use security libraries correctly, and catch risky patterns as they write. Preventing vulnerabilities during development means fewer emergency fixes for maintainers and users.
Memory-safe software.
Moving critical components to memory-safe languages can eliminate common classes of memory errors. AI can help with migrations, with tests and expert review to check behavior and compatibility.
Formal verification.
Mathematical proofs can show that critical components satisfy specified security properties under stated assumptions. Applying these methods to widely used components could help rule out classes of vulnerabilities across the services and critical infrastructure that depend on them.
Stronger isolation.
Systems need protection against vulnerabilities that have not yet been found or fixed. Running software that processes untrusted files, images, or media in tightly restricted sandboxes can limit the damage if it is compromised. AI can help engineers test and strengthen widely used sandboxing technologies, improving protections across many applications.
We’re sharing our approach so maintainers, researchers, response teams, providers, and operators can help make it workable. We want to understand where it would help, where it could add delay or burden, and what we’ve missed. Examples from past disclosures would be especially useful.
We’re particularly interested in:
Disclosure windows.
How much time do maintainers, operators, and defenders need to prepare and deploy reliable fixes? What evidence should trigger earlier disclosure, especially when some users can be protected sooner than others?
Emergency patching.
When, if ever, should governments or other operators use a vulnerability to patch systems they own or are authorized to manage after losing administrative access? What safeguards should apply? We can imagine an end-state of internet-wide always-on scanning for known vulnerabilities, and either notifying site owners when one is detected or patching the system.
Early notice.
Who needs advance warning to take a useful protective action, including defenders we might miss? When do staged notifications work better than notifying a wider group at once, and how should we weigh coverage against leak risk?
Secure-by-default software packages.
How can we help identify and fix vulnerabilities in downstream packages while upstream fixes are pending, or backport fixes to older versions that no longer receive security updates? What testing and maintenance would make these packages reliable options when a timely upgrade isn’t feasible?
We also welcome recommendations for independent security experts who can review the approach, challenge its assumptions, and identify gaps. Your input will help us refine the response and prioritize support.
Share your feedback here
.
2026
Daybreak
Author
Keep reading
View all
Patch the Planet: a Daybreak initiative to support open source maintainers
Jun 22, 2026
Sep 3, 2026
The Defender’s Window
Aug 17, 2026

## OpenAI - Using thousands of AI agents to uncover new leads in biological data Publication Oct 21, 2026

Source: https://openai.com/index/scaling-ai-research-for-complex-biological-data/
Captured: 2026-11-13
Content hash: c17a576894f38ab3eddce8821c6f3ec511bfee327a600d61391b6cb59b2a707c

OpenAI
October 21, 2026
Publication
Research
Using thousands of AI agents to uncover new leads in biological data
How OpenAI, PerturbAI, and Scripps Research used distributed AI agents to help scientists identify promising research leads in data from millions of brain cells
Read the paper
(opens in a new window)
See how OpenAI, PerturbAI, and Scripps Research deployed thousands of AI agents in parallel to identify promising biological connections in complex data from millions of brain cells.
⁠
Video credit: PerturbAI.
Share
Big experiments, unexpected clues
Divide the work, deepen the reasoning
Finding the next experiment worth doing
What if scientists could uncover new clues about Alzheimer’s and Parkinson’s in minutes instead of months? Working together, OpenAI,
PerturbAI
, and
Scripps Research
used
thousands of AI agents powered by GPT‑Rosalind
to analyze and interpret existing experimental data from
millions of mouse brain cells
in just 32 minutes. The agents connected the effects of gene disruptions with findings from scientific literature, uncovering unexpected biological connections that can help scientists understand disease and identify research directions for new treatments. The work shows how AI can help researchers explore more of what large biological experiments reveal.
Modern experiments generate far more data than scientists can thoroughly investigate. Researchers can now alter thousands of genes and measure their effects across millions of cells. But understanding what these results mean for human disease requires connecting complex experimental findings with decades of scattered scientific knowledge. This is where AI can help scientists uncover promising leads that might otherwise be missed.
“We build cell atlases so that other scientists can make discoveries we haven’t imagined yet. AI could help more researchers explore these resources and turn unexpected observations into new questions to test.”
—Sarah Teichmann, Ph.D., Chair in Stem Cell Medicine at the University of Cambridge and founding co-chair of the Human Cell Atlas
The team divided the analysis among thousands of AI agents powered by GPT‑Rosalind, an approach we call distributed biological reasoning. The agents examined a
whole-brain CRISPR atlas
, a dataset showing how gene disruptions affect different types of cells across the mouse brain. Each agent investigated a gene disruption, analyzed cellular responses, searched scientific literature, and proposed explanations for the results. Their reports were combined into a reasoning atlas linking genes, cellular effects, relevant publications, and possible biological mechanisms.
The research team estimated that comparable human analysis and literature review, conducted one perturbation at a time, would take months to a year. The agents reproduced findings from human analyses while uncovering additional leads. Their assessments of novelty and scientific value also aligned with judgments from blinded human experts. These findings help prioritize further experiments needed to test the proposed biological mechanisms.
Some of these insights point to new leads for understanding brain disease. The agents identified unexpected differences in how two disease-linked genes affect brain cells differently, as well as a potential connection between cholesterol regulation and neurodegeneration. These findings could help scientists investigate how disease develops and where future treatments might intervene.
“Sometimes the clues are already there, scattered across different fields—we just haven’t connected them. The possible link between neuronal function and cholesterol metabolism shows how those pieces, once brought together, can give us a new way to think about disease biology.”
—Tomasz Nowakowski, Ph.D., associate professor of neurological surgery at UCSF
Researchers can begin exploring these findings today. The reasoning atlas is
available
, allowing scientists to compare related genes, investigate unexpected responses, and identify promising leads to pursue. New discoveries often emerge when researchers from different fields bring their expertise together and connect familiar ideas in unexpected ways. AI can significantly scale these connections by linking new data with knowledge scattered across disciplines. The opportunity goes beyond analyzing data faster. By combining AI with experimental biology, human expertise, and scientific literature, we can help scientists turn more of their data into testable ideas.
Some of an experiment’s most interesting clues lie outside the story scientists set out to investigate. In a dataset spanning thousands of perturbations, those clues can be easy to miss.
The agents identified an unexpected difference between Tsc1 and Tsc2, two genes that work together to control mTOR, a major regulator of cell growth, metabolism, and aging. Disrupting either gene had similar effects in several groups of neurons. But in the cerebellum, which helps coordinate movement, Tsc2 disruption altered the activity of dozens of genes involved in neuronal communication, while Tsc1 disruption produced little change.
That difference may be relevant to human disease. In people with tuberous sclerosis, abnormalities in the cerebellum are strongly linked to TSC2 mutations. While medicines that reduce mTOR activity can treat certain brain tumors and seizures, learning and behavioral difficulties associated with the disease remain challenging to address. The agents pinpointed a specific group of neurons and cellular changes worth investigating: which changes might existing medicines correct, and which might point to new treatment approaches? This gives researchers a more specific set of questions to test in the lab.
Could a single agent given the entire dataset do just as well as thousands of agents working in parallel? We tested this while keeping the model and resources fixed. As the workload grew, the single agent searched less of the literature, citing only 11 references at the largest workload, while the distributed agents cited more than 700 references. Human experts also preferred the distributed-agent interpretations.
Scientists have limited time and often focus on the strongest signals in their data. Distributed agents can help examine a wider range of responses, including subtle or unexpected ones, and connect them with relevant scientific literature. Running the agents in parallel allows many perturbations to be investigated at once. This helps scientists explore more of what an experiment contains and identify promising leads that might otherwise be missed.
At the largest workload, distributed agents cited more than 700 references, compared with 11 for a single agent.
A finding becomes more valuable when it points to an experiment worth doing. The agents found one such lead in
DCTN1
, a gene whose mutations cause Perry syndrome, a neurodegenerative disease with Parkinson-like symptoms.
DCTN1 is involved in moving cargo inside neurons. In the whole-brain CRISPR atlas, disrupting Dctn1 increased the expression of genes involved in making cholesterol. The agents connected this observation with research suggesting that problems with cholesterol transport may cause cells to make more cholesterol. This raised a question for follow-up experiments: could DCTN1 disruption change how cholesterol is distributed within neurons, and could those changes contribute to neurodegeneration? If so, could restoring normal cholesterol distribution help protect neurons?
Human experts also considered this hypothesis novel, consequential and worth following up on. This example shows how connecting an unexpected result with existing knowledge can turn a clue into a testable idea, and potentially a new direction for treating disease.
In blinded comparisons, human experts preferred distributed-agent reports over single-agent reports 75% of the time.
Scientists remain at the center of discovery. By uncovering more promising leads, AI can help researchers decide which ideas to pursue, design experiments, and interpret results. The goal is to turn more of the information in biological experiments into knowledge that, over time, can help guide the development of better medicines.
2026
Science
Author

FAQ
What is a bot, exactly?
An AI coworker you spin up for one thing: the pipeline that broke, the API migration, the dashboard nobody trusts. It starts generic and becomes yours as you use it. What it learns, what it makes and what you told it all stay with it.
Can my teammates review the same work?
Yes. Invite them into the same bot. They see the same work, correct the assumptions they know are wrong, and the bot keeps going.
What can it see?
Only the data and integrations available to the person who triggered it. A teammate's credentials don't become yours. In a shared bot, everyone who can see the bot can see what it shares, so use a private bot for sensitive work.
How that works →
Whose permissions does a bot use?
A bot has no access of its own. Each time it acts, it borrows the permissions of the person who asked: the data, apps and files that person can already reach, nothing more. If you ask it for a report, it can only pull from what you can see. If a colleague asks the same bot next, it switches to what they can see.
So a shared bot is not a side door. You can't ask it to fetch something a teammate can see and you can't, and neither can they. Access is decided by your organisation's permissions, not by who happens to be in the chat.
One thing to keep in mind: whatever a bot says in a shared chat is visible to everyone in that chat. If the question is sensitive, ask it in a private bot and share the result.
Where does it run?
On its own computer, with its own browser, around the clock. Nothing is installed on your phone or laptop, and it keeps working when you close the app.
Can I work on a sensitive investigation privately?
Yes. Start a private bot for the investigation and share only the reviewed findings with the wider team.
Bot, meet humans.

## Red Hat - Understanding AI/ML use cases

Source: https://www.redhat.com/en/topics/ai/agentic-ai-use-cases
Captured: 2026-11-13
Content hash: 649f1806c072bcbe7adcf299497b7c3b157cefb33f956ab031a14681aa6aeb23

Topics
Artificial intelligence
Understanding agentic AI use cases
Published
August 21, 2026
•
6
-minute read
Copied
Copy failed
Copy URL
Jump to section
What is agentic AI?
Why agentic AI use cases need AgentOps
What is agentic RAG?
Agentic AI use cases
How Red Hat can help
Agentic AI
uses
large language models (LLMs)
and tools to build on the power of
generative AI (gen AI)
. It works by connecting to and communicating with external sources to perceive, decide on, and orchestrate a whole automated task to achieve a defined goal.
To put agentic AI into practice, you create a system that gives an LLM access to external tools and to logic that supplies instructions for how the agentic system should use those tools. An agentic system is a layer that drives and operates other software tools.
Agentic AI can be a physical structure, a software program, or a combination of the 2. For example, an AI agent in a robotic system might use cameras, sensors, and monitors to collect data about its environment, then run that information alongside software to determine its next step. This is a process known as sensor fusion.
In a software setting, agentic AI would collect data from other sources—such as application programming interfaces (APIs), online searches, text prompts, and databases—which helps the agents create a sense of perception and context.
Explore Red Hat AI
Agentic AI vs. AI agents
An AI agent is a noun (“I’m building 3 agents.”). It refers to a software entity built to work and perform a role within an agentic system.
Agentic AI is descriptive and refers to the behavioral characteristics of a system (“We need to make our software more agentic.”). It describes a system that can plan, make decisions, and take action toward goals with limited human guidance.
A brief history (of the brief history) of AI agents
Early iterations of AI agents involved basic API function calling. At that point, AI agents started creating instruction sets for your systems.
Next, frameworks turned a single tool call into a loop: reason, act, observe, and repeat.
Then,
Model Context Protocol (MCP)
came along and standardized communication between applications and services. This made it easier than ever for tools, resources, and prompts to be exposed to and discovered by an MCP client (the component that requests access to external data or resources).
Today, we have autonomous coding agents and multi-agent systems where 1 agent plans tasks and delegates them to subagents it creates on the fly. The human moves from being
in
the loop to being
notified
by the loop.
So, if your agent can act on real systems, with real credentials, over long time horizons, what's stopping it from doing the wrong thing?
AgentOps.
AgentOps
(agent operations) is a framework of tools for monitoring the “brain” of an AI as it makes decisions in real time.
The actions of agents are nondeterministic—that is, decided by a series of random probability distributions. This means we can’t precisely predict their actions. Agentic workflows can help creatively solve problems, but that creativity needs to be managed so systems don’t go rogue.
AgentOps addresses the production layer, making software more reliable. AgentOps helps by:
Tracking hardware resources.
Monitoring hallucination rates.
Ensuring data stays encrypted.
Providing an auditable log of actions made by the agent.
Terminating a process if policy violations occur.
In an enterprise setting, AgentOps is critical for agentic workflows. It provides AI systems that are built on your infrastructure, trained on your data, aligned with your values, and following your rules.
Learn more about AgentOps
4 key considerations for implementing AI technology
Get the resource
Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG)
is a method for getting better answers from a generative AI application by linking an LLM to an external resource. Agentic RAG takes traditional RAG a step further by enabling the LLM to actively investigate rather than simply retrieve.
While RAG can retrieve answers and provide some context from documentation and data sets it has access to, it relies on manual prompt engineering. Traditional RAG also has limited contextual awareness and relies exclusively on the initial query to retrieve relevant information.
Agentic RAG is comparatively more sophisticated and dynamic. It can come up with questions of its own, create context from its memory, and carry out additional tasks without being explicitly asked to do so. This step beyond traditional RAG grants agentic RAG the ability to make more informed decisions on your behalf, independently of your manual intervention.
When models stop answering and start acting
Agentic AI serves many purposes. Let’s take a look at how different industries may use it:
Agentic AI in finance
Financial teams oversee millions of transactions, making manual fraud detection and policy enforcement impossible to keep up with in real time.
Autonomous financial agents can continuously monitor daily transactions and flag for fraud or policy violations before they escalate. Agents ingest live data, cross-reference it with internal rules, and flag anomalies. From here, human reviewers step in to analyze the flagged suspicious activity and make a final judgment call on whether to take action.
Agentic AI in IT and software engineering
Developers spend hours reproducing bugs and writing fixes that could otherwise be spent on building and improving new features. AI agents can help triage by reproducing bugs in a sandbox, drafting potential fixes, and running tests to verify whether the patches work. Once a solution is verified, a human developer reviews code and approves deployment to production.
Agentic AI in supply chain management
External factors like extreme weather, labor strikes, and port congestion can disrupt supply chains, leading to delays and financial losses. Agents can track news updates, monitor weather, and alert teams to potential delays. They could even calculate the financial impact of rerouting. Supply chain managers then review the agent’s cost-benefit analyses and make a final call on which strategy to proceed with.
Agentic AI for autonomous vehicles
Self-driving vehicles have to navigate complex real-world environments, which means interpreting massive streams of conflicting sensory data. An agentic sensor fusion system merges disparate raw input (from cameras and radar) into a cohesive, real-time model of the vehicle's surroundings. The agentic system processes, organizes, and filters data from every vehicle sensor to make sense of visual ambiguities and predict collision risks in milliseconds. Human safety drivers or remote operators oversee edge-case exceptions, monitor the reliability of these systems, and take control manually when necessary.
Agentic AI in communications and public relations
The online world moves faster than any human communications team can be expected to keep up with. Stories break 24x7, sentiment can change in minutes, and a small news story can turn into a crisis quickly.
Agents can spend all day and night tracking global brand mentions across platforms and create a summary to gauge public mood. They can also scan news stories, recently published academic papers, and social trends to compile daily briefings. Then agents can summarize these insights to help teams stay ahead of industry shifts. From these briefs, human communications professionals can craft high-stakes messaging and strategize PR responses for escalated incidents.
Agentic AI in human resources
Onboarding new hires requires repetitive administrative tasks, scheduling, and a constant flow of questions that can drain resources. To assist with this, a dedicated onboarding agent can guide new hires through documentation, IT setup, and training. It can help new hires answer specific questions and schedule introductory meetings based on the employee’s role and department. Freeing up HR leaders’ and team managers’ time lets them focus on high-touch cultural integration, mentorship, and building personal connections with the new hire.
Agentic AI in legal and compliance
Global trade laws and environmental regulations shift all the time, making it easy for international corporations to fall out of compliance. Agents can continuously track regulatory updates worldwide and analyze how changing legal frameworks impact operational workflows. This helps organizations stay ahead of legal risks without requiring manual policy reviews. Once an agent flags a specific area that may need internal adaptation, a human legal counsel can interpret the findings and draft updated corporate policies if needed.
Agentic AI in marketing and sales
In a competitive marketplace, static pricing can lead to lost revenue. Agents monitor competitor pricing and market demand in real time. They suggest price adjustments to maintain an edge while protecting profit margins. Marketing and revenue leaders set pricing strategy boundaries, review automated recommendations, and approve promotional campaigns.
Agentic AI in healthcare
Healthcare workers are overwhelmed with administrative data, which can lead to burnout for the provider and oversights in the patient’s care. A clinical assistant agent can help organize patient data, flag missing lab results, and alert the team to potential medication conflicts or dosage issues. Doctors and clinical staff treat the agent as a safety net, reviewing the flagged alerts and spending more time delivering direct patient care.
Explore agentic use cases with Red Hat
Red Hat® AI
operationalizes the full lifecycle of an agent through a dedicated AgentOps control plane. This helps keep deployments safeguarded, observable, and efficient across your hybrid cloud environment.
The platform provides enterprise-grade governance through integrated safety guardrails. Its underlying infrastructure uses
vLLM
and
llm‑d
for high-performance
distributed inference
, so you can scale resource-intensive workflows, from on-premise to edge environments.
Red Hat AI offers fast, flexible, and efficient inference through its vLLM-powered server. It reliably connects models to your data to unify the customization and development of specialized agents on a single platform. Built on an open source foundation, our AI products give you full control of AI workflows from end to end at any scale.

How LifeMD Saved 475,000+ Hours With AI Agents
A leading virtual healthcare provider used StackAI to automate patient support and streamline clinical operations—executing over 2.5 million AI agent actions, saving 475,000 hours, and cutting support wait times to just 12 seconds.
A leading virtual healthcare provider partnered with StackAI to transform patient support, streamline operations, and improve the quality of care. With over 2.5 million AI agent executions and 475,000 hours saved, the organization is reshaping how healthcare is delivered at scale. Using StackAI’s secure, no-code platform, the provider built and deployed AI agents that now power everything from customer support to clinical operations—all while maintaining HIPAA compliance and enterprise-grade data privacy.
475,000+ physician hours saved by automating patient data retrieval
12 second average support wait time, down from minutes
2.5M+ AI agent tasks executed securely across the organization
The Opportunity: Meeting Complex Needs with Limited Resources
In healthcare, every patient comes with unique needs, and the stakes are higher than in almost any other industry. To meet those needs, this virtual healthcare provider offers primary, specialty, and urgent care services via telehealth, and partners with thousands of licensed professionals across the U.S. to deliver timely, reliable care.
But the organization was under pressure, as a growing volume of patient inquiries, appointments, and account management tasks overwhelmed support teams. Every phone call, message, and insurance question had to be addressed with medical accuracy, regulatory compliance, and personal sensitivity. Traditional support programs simply couldn’t scale.
On top of this complexity, healthcare data is highly sensitive. The healthcare provider needed a solution that wouldn’t just move fast, but would meet strict HIPAA and PII standards, and fit into their existing infrastructure. That’s when they began to explore agentic AI, landing on StackAI to help transform their patient support workflows and more.
Instant, Accurate Patient Support
The Problem: Overwhelmed Call Centers
Support teams were swamped with inquiries about symptoms, billing, and insurance. Every answer had to be medically accurate, compliant, and patient-specific—but manually finding information in health record systems was slow and error-prone.
The Solution: AI-Powered Support Agent
With StackAI, the provider built a secure agent connected to its EHR system. Agents now retrieve patient history, cross-reference documentation, and generate accurate, HIPAA-compliant responses in seconds.
Support wait times dropped to 12 seconds, with agents used over 20,000 times per week.
This AI agent didn’t just help reps respond faster, it improved care quality by providing answers grounded in patient context. StackAI’s HIPAA-compliance added an additional layer of security.
Expanding AI Across the Organization: Helping Physicians Provide Better Care
The Problem: Manual Data Retrieval Slowed Care
Physicians spent hours retrieving lab results, notes, and prescriptions across multiple systems, delaying consultations and reducing focus on patient interaction.
The Solution: Physician AI Assistant
Doctors now query a dedicated AI assistant that instantly compiles a patient’s full medical history. This ensures faster, more informed decisions and frees up physician time for care delivery.
Over 475,000 physician hours have been saved to date, with this AI agent being used 100,000+ times per week.
Compliance at Scale
The Problem: Risk of Non-Compliance in Support Calls
Every recorded support call had to be reviewed for regulatory compliance and proper disclosures—a massive manual burden that exposed the provider to legal and reputational risk.
The Solution: AI-Powered Call Audit Agent
A StackAI agent now classifies, summarizes, and flags potential compliance gaps across thousands of calls weekly. This automated audit ensures consistency, reduces exposure, and builds trust with patients.
This AI agent runs 55,000+ times per week, dramatically reducing compliance risk and QA workload.
“The StackAI team has been incredibly responsive. Anytime we’ve had unique requests, whether around integrations, model deployment options, or scaling considerations... they’ve handled them quickly. It’s rare to see that level of support, especially for something this technically deep."
Stefan Gallupi
Chief Innovation Officer, LifeMD
475,000 Hours Saved. 12 Second Wait Times. What’s Next?
Since launch, the healthcare provider has used StackAI to execute over 2.5 million AI agent tasks. Physicians alone have saved over 475,000 hours previously lost to data retrieval and manual workflows. Average wait times for call support are now just 12 seconds. The team is now looking to design and deploy an agent to run patient support across their website automatically.
By expanding across departments, with an eye on security and safety for sensitive patient data, this healthcare provider has turned AI agents into a helpful, pragmatic pillar of its patient experience strategy. From support to clinical ops, StackAI is helping this organization meet modern healthcare’s demands with speed, safety, and scale.

FAQs
What is a Vellum assistant?
Vellum is a personal, secure AI assistant that you fully own and customize to your needs. It runs locally on your machine or in the cloud, is available around the clock from any device, remembers you over time, manages tasks across your tools, and handles work for you automatically.
How is my Vellum assistant different from ChatGPT or Claude?
ChatGPT and Claude are chat windows that only act on command, train off your data, and have limited memory. Vellum is an autonomous assistant with long-term memory that takes real actions across your files and tools. Your personal data stays private, none of your data accessible by Vellum unless you opt in, and you can switch between models including OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini, or open weights via Ollama at any time.
What can my Vellum assistant do?
It can send emails, manage your calendar, browse the web, create slides, write code, run deep research, monitor websites, and execute recurring tasks in the background. It comes with built-in skills for Google Workspace, Slack, Notion, and your filesystem, and can be extended with community plugins or your own custom skills.
Can my Vellum assistant do things without asking me?
Only what you allow it to. Vellum has a fine-grained permissions system built to protect your data. High-stakes actions like sending emails to new contacts, modifying important files, or spending credits require explicit approval until you mark them as trusted. You are always in control.
What can I use my Vellum assistant on?
Vellum is native to Mac and accessible everywhere: iOS, Android, web app, voice, email, Telegram, Slack, and terminal. Your assistant shares the same memory, active tasks, and schedules across every surface.
Is the data from my Vellum assistant private?
Yes. By default, your assistant runs locally. Your conversation history, memory, and credentials stay on your machine and are never used to train models. Vellum is open-source, so you can inspect the code yourself. If you use Vellum Cloud, your assistant runs in an isolated container provisioned exclusively for you.
How much does my Vellum assistant cost?
Vellum is free to download and start. If you choose Vellum Cloud, subscription plans start at $30/month (Mighty), $100/month (Super), or $200/month (Ultra) with dedicated compute, storage, and bundled credits. Compare every plan on the
pricing plans
page.

Our pricing philosophy
We believe your AI costs should be transparent and predictable. Vellum passes through model provider costs at cost. We don't charge any margin or markup on token usage. When you spend $1 worth of credits on LLM tokens, that full dollar goes to the model provider. Our goal is to keep Vellum affordable and aligned with your actual usage, not to profit from the AI calls your assistant makes.
Plans
Vellum has two plans:
Base
(free) and
Pro
(paid). Pro comes in three preset packages (Mighty, Super, Ultra) that bundle machine size, storage, and monthly usage, or you can build a
Custom
configuration by selecting each component individually.
Free, forever. Includes everything you need to run an assistant:
Small machine size (1 vCPU, 2 GiB RAM)
6 GiB of persistent storage
Pay-as-you-go credits (no monthly minimum)
Managed LLM credentials (Vellum covers the API keys)
Pro packages
Three preset packages that bundle a machine size, storage tier, and monthly usage allowance into a single monthly price. Each package is a starting point: you can adjust individual tiers afterward, which converts your plan to a Custom configuration.

is the entry-level Pro package. You get 10 GiB of storage and monthly Mighty Usage on the standard Small machine. The $10/mo platform fee is not included, so there is no custom subdomain, static IP, or priority support.

Prefer to pick your own components? The Custom plan lets you select a machine size, storage tier, and optional usage bundle individually. Your monthly total is the sum of:

The minimum Custom configuration is $50/mo (Medium machine + 10 GiB storage, no usage bundle). Credits are still pay-as-you-go on top of any bundle you choose. If you start on a package and later change any individual tier, your plan automatically becomes Custom.

Sets the maximum compute for assistants in your org. The Small machine is included with Base and the Mighty package. Medium, Large, and XL are available on the Custom plan and the Super and Ultra packages. Resizable anytime from the assistant's settings page.
